<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:3rd_class_relic_PIO.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/3rd_class_relic_PIO.jpg/220px-3rd_class_relic_PIO.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="114" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/3rd_class_relic_PIO.jpg/330px-3rd_class_relic_PIO.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/3rd_class_relic_PIO.jpg/440px-3rd_class_relic_PIO.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3620" data-file-height="1869" /></a><figcaption>Many books about Padre Pio included a <a href="/wiki/Relic" title="Relic">third-class relic</a> (cloth) on a prayer card. This relic was encased when he was considered "<a href="/wiki/Servant_of_God#Catholic_Church" title="Servant of God">Venerable</a>", but he has since been <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonized</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1982, the Holy See authorized the <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Manfredonia" class="mw-redirect" title="Archbishop of Manfredonia">archbishop of Manfredonia</a> to open an investigation to determine whether Pio should be canonized. The investigation continued for seven years. In 1990, Pio was declared a <a href="/wiki/Servant_of_God" title="Servant of God">Servant of God</a>, the first step in the process of canonization. The investigation, however, did not lead to any public factual clearance by the Church on the allegations that his stigmata were not of a supernatural kind. Moreover, Pio's stigmata were remarkably left out of the obligatory investigations for the canonization process, in order to avoid obstacles prohibiting a successful closure.
</p><p>Beginning in 1990, the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Causes_of_Saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Causes of Saints">Congregation for the Causes of Saints</a> debated how Padre Pio had lived his life, and in 1997 <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a> declared him <a href="/wiki/Venerable" class="mw-redirect" title="Venerable">venerable</a>. A discussion of the effects of his life on others followed. Cases were studied such as a reported cure of an Italian woman, Consiglia de Martino, associated with Padre Pio's <a href="/wiki/Intercession" title="Intercession">intercession</a>. In 1999, on the advice of the Congregation, John Paul II declared Padre Pio blessed and set 23 September as the date of his liturgical feast.<sup id="cite_ref-biovatican_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-biovatican-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The mass for his beatification was celebrated on 2 May 1999 by John Paul II on <a href="/wiki/St._Peter%27s_Square" title="St. Peter&#39;s Square">St. Peter's Square</a> in <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a>, with more than 300,000 faithful attending the ceremony.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> In his <a href="/wiki/Homily" title="Homily">homily</a>, John Paul II mentioned Padre Pio's stigmata and his mystical gifts:<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>"His body, marked by the 'stigmata', showed forth the intimate bond between death and resurrection which characterizes the paschal mystery. Bl. Pio of Pietrelcina shared in the Passion with a special intensity: the unique gifts which were given to him, and the interior and mystical sufferings which accompanied them, allowed him constantly to participate in the Lord's agonies, never wavering in his sense that 'Calvary is the hill of the saints'."</p></blockquote>
<p>After his beatification, another case of healing attributed to his intercession was examined, an Italian boy named Matteo Pio Colella who recovered from a coma<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> After further consideration of Padre Pio's virtues and ability to do good even after his death, John Paul II promulgated the decree of canonization on 28 February 2002.<sup id="cite_ref-biovatican_8-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-biovatican-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> The mass for the canonization was celebrated by John Paul II on 16 June 2002 on St. Peter's Square in Rome, with an estimated 300,000 people attending the ceremony.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Pilgrimage_sites">Pilgrimage sites</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Padre_Pio&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Pilgrimage sites">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Chiesa_San_Pio_da_Pietrelcina.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b4/Chiesa_San_Pio_da_Pietrelcina.JPG/220px-Chiesa_San_Pio_da_Pietrelcina.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b4/Chiesa_San_Pio_da_Pietrelcina.JPG/330px-Chiesa_San_Pio_da_Pietrelcina.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b4/Chiesa_San_Pio_da_Pietrelcina.JPG/440px-Chiesa_San_Pio_da_Pietrelcina.JPG 2x" data-file-width="4000" data-file-height="3000" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Sanctuary_of_Saint_Pio_of_Pietrelcina" title="Sanctuary of Saint Pio of Pietrelcina">Sanctuary of Saint Pio of Pietrelcina</a> in <a href="/wiki/San_Giovanni_Rotondo" title="San Giovanni Rotondo">San Giovanni Rotondo</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The town of <a href="/wiki/San_Giovanni_Rotondo" title="San Giovanni Rotondo">San Giovanni Rotondo</a>, where Padre Pio spent most of his life, is the main pilgrimage site dedicated to his memory. The Santa Maria delle Grazie Church, which is the church of the capuchin monastery where Padre Pio celebrated mass, has become a pilgrimage site for his followers after his death in 1968. As the number of pilgrims kept increasing over the years, the Capuchins decided to build a new shrine near the church. The construction of the shrine began in 1991 and was completed in 2004.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> On 1 July 2004, John Paul II dedicated the <a href="/wiki/Sanctuary_of_Saint_Pio_of_Pietrelcina" title="Sanctuary of Saint Pio of Pietrelcina">Sanctuary of Saint Pio of Pietrelcina</a>, sometimes referred as the <i>Padre Pio Pilgrimage Church</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> The sanctuary has a capacity of around 6,000 people and its parvis has a 30,000 capacity.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> The relics of Padre Pio are located in the crypt of the new sanctuary and displayed for veneration by the pilgrims.
</p><p>The town of <a href="/wiki/Pietrelcina" title="Pietrelcina">Pietrelcina</a>, where Padre Pio grew up, is another pilgrimage site which became popular among devotees. The sites which can be visited by pilgrims in Pietrelcina include Pio's family house where he was born, his room in an ancient tower in which he stayed as a friar when he was ill, the Santa Anna Church where he was baptized, the Santa Maria degli Angeli Church, where he was ordained a deacon prior to becoming a priest, and the Capuchin Church of the Holy Family. It is estimated that around two millions pilgrims come to Pietrelcina every year.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Sanctuaries and places dedicated to the veneration of Padre Pio outside Italy include the <a href="/wiki/Padre_Pio_Shrine" title="Padre Pio Shrine">Padre Pio Shrine</a> in <a href="/wiki/Santo_Tomas,_Batangas" title="Santo Tomas, Batangas">Santo Tomas, Batangas</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Philippines" title="Philippines">Philippines</a> and the National Center for Padre Pio in <a href="/wiki/Barto,_Pennsylvania" title="Barto, Pennsylvania">Barto, Pennsylvania</a>, in the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Veneration_by_the_popes">Veneration by the popes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Padre_Pio&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Veneration by the popes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Popes have encouraged popular devotion to Padre Pio in various ways, notably by visiting the places associated with his life and ministry. <a href="/wiki/San_Giovanni_Rotondo" title="San Giovanni Rotondo">San Giovanni Rotondo</a>, where Padre Pio spent most of his life and where his shrine is located, was visited by <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pope_Francis" title="Pope Francis">Pope Francis</a>. <a href="/wiki/Pietrelcina" title="Pietrelcina">Pietrelcina</a>, Padre Pio's birthplace, was visited by Pope Francis.
</p><p>Pope John Paul II had much admiration for Padre Pio even before he became Pope, when Padre Pio was still alive. In 1947, as a young priest studying in Rome, Karol Wojtyła made a pilgrimage to San Giovanni Rotondo to meet Padre Pio in person. He returned to San Giovanni Rotondo as a cardinal, in 1974. He visited San Giovanni Rotondo again in May 1987 as Pope John Paul II, to celebrate the 100th anniversary of Padre Pio's birth.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 21 June 2009, Pope Benedict XVI visited San Giovanni Rotondo as a pastoral visit. He visited the Santa Maria delle Grazie Church to venerate of the relics of Padre Pio in the crypt, celebrated mass and met with various people, including with the sick and the employees of the <a href="/wiki/Casa_Sollievo_della_Sofferenza" title="Casa Sollievo della Sofferenza">Casa Sollievo della Sofferenza</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pope Francis has also supported popular devotion to Padre Pio. During the <a href="/wiki/Extraordinary_Jubilee_of_Mercy" title="Extraordinary Jubilee of Mercy">Extraordinary Jubilee of Mercy</a>, he requested that Padre Pio's relics be exposed for veneration in St. Peter's Basilica from 8 to 14 February 2016 and he specially honored Padre Pio as a "saint-confessors" to inspire people to resort to the <a href="/wiki/Sacrament_of_Penance" title="Sacrament of Penance">sacrament of Penance</a> during the Jubilee.<sup id="cite_ref-groups_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-groups-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> On 17 March 2018, Pope Francis visited both Pietrelcina and San Giovanni Rotondo to celebrate the 50th anniversary of Padre Pio's death.<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Exhumation">Exhumation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Padre_Pio&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Exhumation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>On 3 March 2008, the body of Pio was exhumed from his crypt, forty years after his death, so that his remains could be prepared for display. A church statement described the body as being in "fair condition". <a href="/wiki/Archbishop" title="Archbishop">Archbishop</a> Domenico Umberto D'Ambrosio, <a href="/wiki/Papal_legate" title="Papal legate">Papal legate</a> to the shrine in San Giovanni Rotondo, stated "the top part of the skull is partly skeletal but the chin is perfect and the rest of the body is well preserved".<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> Archbishop D'Ambrosio also confirmed in a communiqué that "the stigmata are not visible".<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> He said that Pio's hands "looked like they had just undergone a manicure". It was hoped that <a href="/wiki/Morticians" class="mw-redirect" title="Morticians">morticians</a> would be able to restore the face so that it would be recognizable. However, because of its deterioration, his face was covered with a lifelike <a href="/wiki/Silicone" title="Silicone">silicone</a> mask.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> This mask was made from a 1968 photograph of Padre Pio's body by the London-based Gems Studio, which usually works for wax museums and ethnological museums.<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Saraiva_Martins" title="José Saraiva Martins">José Saraiva Martins</a>, prefect for the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Causes_of_the_Saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Causes of the Saints">Congregation for the Causes of the Saints</a>, celebrated <a href="/wiki/Mass_(liturgy)" title="Mass (liturgy)">Mass</a> for 15,000 devotees on April 24 at the Shrine of Holy Mary of Grace, San Giovanni Rotondo, before the body went on display in a crystal, marble, and silver <a href="/wiki/Sepulcher" class="mw-redirect" title="Sepulcher">sepulcher</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Crypt" title="Crypt">crypt</a> of the monastery.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> Padre Pio is wearing his brown Capuchin habit with a white silk <a href="/wiki/Stole_(vestment)" title="Stole (vestment)">stole</a> embroidered with crystals and gold thread. His hands hold a large wooden cross. 800,000 <a href="/wiki/Pilgrims" class="mw-redirect" title="Pilgrims">pilgrims</a> worldwide, mostly from Italy, made reservations to view the body up to December 2008, but only 7,200 people a day were able to file past the crystal coffin.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> Officials extended the display through September 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pio's remains were placed in the <a href="/wiki/Sanctuary_of_Saint_Pio_of_Pietrelcina" title="Sanctuary of Saint Pio of Pietrelcina">church of Saint Pio</a>, which is beside San Giovanni Rotondo. In April 2010 they were moved to a special golden crypt.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Prayer_groups">Prayer groups</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Padre_Pio&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Prayer groups">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Padre Pio's prayer groups began in the 1950s when <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_XII" title="Pope Pius XII">Pope Pius XII</a> called to form groups gathering monthly to pray together.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAllegri2000270_113-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAllegri2000270-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> Padre Pio responded to this call and encouraged his devotees to form such groups. The groups were associated to the construction of the <i><a href="/wiki/Casa_Sollievo_della_Sofferenza" title="Casa Sollievo della Sofferenza">Casa Sollievo della Sofferenza</a></i>, with a biweekly bulletin named after the hospital and published from 1949, which helped these group develop.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERega2005223_114-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERega2005223-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> In 1951, the first statutes of the prayer groups were codified.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERega2005224_115-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERega2005224-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> The prayer groups gathered monthly, with the agreement of the local bishop and under the guidance of a priest, to pray for the pope and for Padre Pio's ministries.<sup id="cite_ref-groups_101-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-groups-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> New statutes were formally approved by the Vatican in 1986.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERega2005223_114-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERega2005223-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> Padre Pio's prayer groups are coordinated from their headquarters in the <i>Casa Sollievo della Sofferenza</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-groups_101-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-groups-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The prayer groups kept growing during Padre Pio's life and after his death. In 1968 at Padre Pio's death, there were around 700 groups, with 68,000 members in 15 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERega2005223_114-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERega2005223-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> In 2013, there were approximately 3,300 registered groups in 60 countries, with about 75% of those groups based in Italy and 25% outside of Italy, mainly in <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ireland" title="Ireland">Ireland</a> and the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-groups_101-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-groups-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Worldwide_devotion">Worldwide devotion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Padre_Pio&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Worldwide devotion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Padre Pio has become one of the world's most popular saints.<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> There are more than 3,000 "Padre Pio Prayer Groups" worldwide, with three million members. The first St Padre Pio parish in the world was established 16 June 2002 in Kleinburg, Ontario, Canada.<sup id="cite_ref-stpp_117-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-stpp-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> There are <a href="/wiki/Parish" title="Parish">parishes</a> in <a href="/wiki/Vineland,_New_Jersey" title="Vineland, New Jersey">Vineland</a> and <a href="/wiki/Lavallette,_New_Jersey" title="Lavallette, New Jersey">Lavallette, New Jersey</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Sydney" title="Sydney">Sydney</a>, <a href="/wiki/Australia" title="Australia">Australia</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Shrine" title="Shrine">shrines</a> in <a href="/wiki/St._Padre_Pio_Shrine" title="St. Padre Pio Shrine">Buena</a>, <a href="/wiki/New_Jersey" title="New Jersey">New Jersey</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Santo_Tomas,_Batangas" title="Santo Tomas, Batangas">Santo Tomas, Batangas</a>, <a href="/wiki/Philippines" title="Philippines">Philippines</a>, dedicated to Padre Pio. A 2006 survey by the magazine <i><a href="/wiki/Famiglia_Cristiana" title="Famiglia Cristiana">Famiglia Cristiana</a></i> found that more Italian Catholics pray to Padre Pio for <a href="/wiki/Intercession" title="Intercession">intercession</a> than to any other figure.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2002, a few months after the canonization of Padre Pio, a new TV station was launched in Italy dedicated to his life and devotion, named <a href="/wiki/Padre_Pio_TV" title="Padre Pio TV">Padre Pio TV</a>. It is based at San Giovanni Rotondo and broadcasts on its own website and various internet platforms.<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The remains of Saint Pio were brought to the Vatican for veneration during the 2015–2016 <a href="/wiki/Extraordinary_Jubilee_of_Mercy" title="Extraordinary Jubilee of Mercy">Extraordinary Jubilee of Mercy</a>. Saint Pio and Saint <a href="/wiki/Leopold_Mandic" class="mw-redirect" title="Leopold Mandic">Leopold Mandic</a> were designated as saint-confessors to inspire people to become reconciled to the Church and to God, by the confession of their sins.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Saint Pio of Pietrelcina was named the patron saint of civil defence volunteers, after a group of 160 petitioned the Italian Bishops' conference for this designation. The bishops forwarded the request to the Vatican, which gave its approval to the designation.<sup id="cite_ref-Patron_121-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Patron-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> He is also "less officially" known as the patron saint of stress relief and the January blues", after the Catholic Enquiry Office in London proclaimed him as such. They designated the most depressing day of the year, identified as the Monday closest to January 22, as <i>Don't Worry Be Happy Day</i>, in honor of Padre Pio's famous advice:  "Pray, hope, and don't worry."<sup id="cite_ref-bbc_2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Iconography">Iconography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Padre_Pio&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Iconography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Padre Pio's iconography has been widely reproduced on devotional items and statues throughout Italy and the world, even before his beatification and canonization. His portrait can be seen in Italy in many churches as well as in private homes and public places, such as shops restaurants.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc_2-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> In religious art, he is usually depicted in his brown Capuchin habit with gloves covering his <a href="/wiki/Stigmata" title="Stigmata">stigmata</a>.
</p><p>Statues of Padre Pio have been erected in Italy and in other countries, including in the United States, the Philippines and Malta.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> In the <a href="/wiki/St._Padre_Pio_Shrine" title="St. Padre Pio Shrine">St. Padre Pio Shrine</a> in <a href="/wiki/Landisville,_New_Jersey" title="Landisville, New Jersey">Landisville, New Jersey</a>, there is a statue of Padre Pio built in and imported from Italy.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> A statue of Pio in <a href="/wiki/Messina" title="Messina">Messina</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sicily" title="Sicily">Sicily</a>, attracted attention in 2002 when it supposedly wept tears of blood.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> In Italy, near the coast of the <a href="/wiki/Capraia" title="Capraia">Capraia</a> island in the <a href="/wiki/Mediterranean_Sea" title="Mediterranean Sea">Mediterranean Sea</a>, there is an underwater statue of Padre Pio at 40 feet (13 meters) deep, submerged in 1998.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2021, the construction of a new sanctuary dedicated to Padre Pio started on a hill overlooking <a href="/wiki/Cebu_City" title="Cebu City">Cebu City</a>, in the Philippines, with a 100-foot-tall statue of Padre Pio.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> The same year a statue of Padre Pio was inaugurated in the <a href="/wiki/Padre_Pio_Shrine" title="Padre Pio Shrine">Padre Pio Shrine</a> in <a href="/wiki/Santo_Tomas,_Batangas" title="Santo Tomas, Batangas">Santo Tomas, Batangas</a>, Philippines.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
